We proceed with the examination of item 8 of the agenda: the draft resolution presented by the Sejm Bureau on the commemoration of Ernest Malinowski's 200th anniversary (print No 3096). I would ask your fellow Members to rise. [[The Gathered Rise Up]] I'll read the draft resolution. The resolution of the Polish Sejm on commemorating the 200th anniversary of Ernest Malinowski's birth. 200 years ago, on January 5, 1818, a family with deep independence traditions was born a recognized road and rail engineer Ernest Malinowski. In Peru is celebrated the Year of Commemoration of the 200th anniversary of the Birth of an outstanding Pole who entered the history of this country, becoming his national hero. Ernest Malinowski's greatest achievement, thanks to which he gained world fame, was the design and construction of the Trans-Indian railway. With nearly 220 km of railway line, it rises to this day almost 5,000 metres above sea level, being a breakthrough achievement of the technique of that time. All the most important technical journals in the world wrote about this railroad, especially the bridges and tunnels on its route. Until 2006, it was the highest railway line in the world. Ernest Malinowski as a Honorary citizen of Peru, working for this country for 43 years, unable to return to his homeland, was and is still present in the memory of Peruvians. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland joins in celebrating the 200th anniversary of the birth of our outstanding Rodak, expressing appreciation for his attitude and professional achievements, which he has entered into the world's cultural and technical heritage. I ask the High House to adopt a resolution by acclamation. [[Applause]] Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-12-sejm-74-1.u366	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! Dear guests! I have the honour to present, on behalf of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, a draft law amending the Police Act and some other laws, the printing of the Polish Parliament VIII term no. 3088. The draft was initially discussed at the meeting of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs on 10 December. The draft is the execution of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 14 December 2017, sygn. Act K 17/14, in which the Constitutional Tribunal states that Article. 219 § 2 of the Act of 6 June 1997 Code of Criminal Procedure and Art. 44 § 2 of the Law of 24 August 2001. The Code of conduct in cases of misconduct in so far as the search of a person is envisaged, without specifying the boundaries of this search, is incompatible with Article. Article 41 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 47 in conjunction with Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. In addition, the Constitutional Tribunal states that the provisions of the implementing acts adopted pursuant to the laws of the Police, the Border Guard, the municipal guards, the police and military law enforcement bodies, the Internal Security Agency and the Intelligence Agency, the Military Counterintelligence Service and the Military Intelligence Service, the Central Anti-corruption Office, the Prison Service, the foreigners, the State Protection Service and the Marshal's Guard are for formal reasons incompatible with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The draft sets out in detail the relevant articles cited in the Act. The Constitution of the Republic of Poland in Chapter: Freedoms and Personal Rights contains a wide range of rights and guarantees which concern the personal freedom of every citizen. On the content of the judgment of the Constitutional Court of 2017 recalling the article. 41, 47, 31, 45 and 77 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland makes it clear that it is necessary to adapt the provisions in the provisions set out in the laws governing the definition of rules, procedures and limits of searches and the powers of officers to carry out personal checks, and to adapt them to the constitutional standards expressed in particular in the Article. 41 (1), Article 4 (1) (a) (ii) (ii) (ii) (iii) (ii) (iii) (ii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii)) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iv) (iv) (iv) (iv) (iv) (iv) (iv) (iv) (iv) (iv) (iv) 47 in conjunction with Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3, Article Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2nd The articles of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland set out in the statement of reasons for the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal guarantee immunity and personal freedom for every citizen and state that the limitation of that right can only be done in accordance with the rules and procedures laid down in the Act. Art. 47 The Constitution guarantees everyone the right to protect their private, family life, honor and good name and to decide on their personal life. The restriction of constitutional freedoms can only be imposed by law. A restriction on the use of constitutional freedoms can only be achieved if it is necessary for the sake of security, public order, protection of public health and morals, freedom and rights of others. According to Art. 45 Constitutions each have the right to a fair and public hearing by an independent, impartial and independent court. No one can be shut down on a judicial basis in the investigation of violated freedoms and rights. The high standards of protection of the freedoms and rights of citizens contained in the Constitution of the Republic of Poland are also reflected in the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 14 December 2017, which, in the broad and multilateral condition of freedom and rights of the individual, recognises the activities performed by service officers within the framework of their powers. The activities carried out in the context of the personal inspection of the citizen shall be equivalent to those carried out in breach of the personal immunity protected by Article. 45 and Article 45 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to The Ombudsman made a request to the Constitutional Court to regulate the powers of officials to carry out personal checks and searches in accordance with the Constitution. The Ombudsman's application to the Constitutional Tribunal is not contested. The Ombudsman's request is a formal part of the regulation which consists of an unconstitutional level of regulation from the point of view of the protection of constitutional rights and freedoms. These regulations are contained in regulations and regulations, whereas the constitution requires statutory regulation of these acts. The current powers of the police and others listed in the act of the security services of citizens and states concerning personal inspection, i.e. activities which enter into the sphere of personal freedoms and the rights of citizens, are not the result of the law, but of regulations and regulations. The subject matter of the Commission's draft law shall be the activities carried out by service officers within the framework of their powers. These activities are complex and ambiguous and therefore difficult to express clearly in the strict language of the law. This is confirmed by the Constitutional Tribunal, which, in its statement of reasons, refers to several of its own judgments concerning the protection of the freedoms and rights of citizens issued in 2000 and 2006, indicating the difficulty of taking the matter into account clearly and in detail in the language of law. At the same time, the Constitutional Tribunal confirms and indicates the necessary inclusion in the rules of law of service proceedings in matters affecting constitutional freedoms and rights, so as not to replace the provisions of the Act with the application of implementing rules. The draft law submitted in print No 3088 fulfils the conditions laid down in the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 14 December 2017, sygn. Act K 17/14, OJ U. poz. 2405. The contested law transfers the scope of the verification activities in question to the relevant laws, clearly clarifies the principles and procedures of the statutory limitation of immunity and personal liberty, as defined in general by Article. 41 constitutions. The constitutional principle of judicial review of the validity, legality and regularity of personal checks, which is important for the protection of rights and freedoms, is introduced into the laws. The provisions introduced by the contested draft law implement the provisions of the 2017 Constitutional Court judgment, order the manner and principles of protection of the rights and freedoms of citizens, define the powers of officials to carry out personal checks and the limits of the search, aligning the activities carried out with the constitutional standards expressed in Article. Article 41 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1, Article 47 in conjunction with Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 and Article 4 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 1). Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. On behalf of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, I would like to thank all persons and institutions who have contributed significantly to adapting the content of the laws to constitutional standards and thus to establishing high standards for the protection of the freedoms and rights of citizens guaranteed in the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. High standards of protection of citizens' freedoms and rights, high standards related to the possibility to challenge the rights infringed, if any, are reflected in the Constitution. In this case, on the basis of this law, there is a provision in the Act for this appeal to be addressed through the Police Unit to the court. Of course, the implementing acts will include models of such a protocol so that the protocols are uniform throughout the legal system. This court is ultimately to give code protection in terms of how the search is carried out, but it is also to determine the validity and purpose of such searches. Judicial review will also be carried out with regard to the screening of baggage content, the checking of cargo in ports and stations and the means of land, air and water transport. ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-12-sejm-74-1.u383	Thank you, sir. The list of Members registered in the vote has been exhausted. Please answer the questions raised by the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration of Ms Renata Szczech.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-12-sejm-74-1.u384	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In my first words, I would like to thank the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs very much for taking the legislative initiative aimed at implementing the Constitutional Tribunal judgment of 14 December 2017. The rightness of the proposed regulations is without doubt due to an increase in the standard of constitutional protection of civil rights and freedoms - mentioned here by Members - as well as in terms of the regularity and legality of the service. In view of the above criteria, the Commission's draft law amending the Police Act and certain other laws deserves support in the assessment of the Ministry of Home Affairs and Administration submitted for consideration. In the judgment referred to at the outset, the Constitutional Tribunal held that the provisions of the Code of Criminal Procedure and the Code of Conduct in Criminal Procedure in so far as they provide for the search of a person, without specifying the boundaries of this search, are incompatible with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The second category of provisions challenged by the Court are the provisions of the pragmatic laws - the Police, the Border Guard, the Government Protection Office, the Military Police and the Military Enforcement Authorities, the Internal Security Agency and the Intelligence Agency, the Military Counterintelligence Service and the Military Intelligence Service, the Central Anti-corruption Office and the Municipal Guards - as well as the implementing acts issued on their basis to the extent that they empower officials, municipal guards and soldiers to carry out personal checks, without specifying the limits of that control, and to the extent that they do not provide for judicial checks on compliance with the law of its exercise. At this point I would like to point out that as regards Mr Wójcik's question about the powers of municipal guards, they are precisely covered by the provisions of the law governing the powers of municipal guards. As regards the question concerning security companies, this is not covered by the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. In examining the proposed solutions, it should be borne in mind that both the Ombudsman in his application initiating the case and the Constitutional Tribunal in the judgment cited above did not question either the powers of the servants to carry out searches and personal checks, nor the catalogue of situations in which these activities may be undertaken. As has already been mentioned, only the formal side of the regulation, which is insufficient - from the constitutional point of view of the protection of freedom and human and citizen rights - to the extent of the detail of the statutory regulations relating to search and personal inspection, has been questioned. I would like to point out here that, given the extent of the necessary changes as set out in the Court's ruling, the draft sets out the scope of the actual actions, which include the exercise of personal checks, the manner in which it is carried out, the duties and powers of the officer, the municipal guard or the officer carrying out the inspection, as well as the way in which these activities are documented. The scope of personal inspection activities is defined taking into account the principle of proportionality. It was also pointed out that this action consisted in checking the contents of the clothing and footwear of the person under personal inspection and the items on his body, without revealing the covered clothing of the body surface, as well as checking the contents of the hand luggage and other items which he carries with him. Only in cases where, during the verification referred to earlier, the possession of weapons or other dangerous objects which may be used to commit acts prohibited under the threat of punishment or of objects which are prohibited may the contents of the clothing and footwear of the person covered by the clothing of the body be checked. That's what I'm trying to clarify. In addition, from the point of view of the protection of rights and freedoms, a clause requiring the limitation of the scope of personal control-related interference, according to which the activities must be carried out in a manner that least infringes the personal interests of the controlled person and to the extent necessary for the purpose of control in the circumstances concerned. Thus, the personal inspection will not in any case involve all legally defined activities, but only those which, in a particular case, will be necessary to achieve the objective of those activities. The rules and procedures for carrying out checks have also been detailed, indicating that a personal inspection is carried out by an officer of the same sex as the person checked and in a place not accessible to outsiders when carrying out the checks. A derogation in this respect shall only be possible if the inspection must be carried out immediately, in particular because of circumstances liable to endanger human life, health or property. It is also provided that during personal inspection a person may be present by the performing person and, where this does not prevent or significantly impede personal inspection, also the person indicated by the controlled person. The proposed regulations indicate that a controlled person shall be entitled to a complaint before the regional court competent for the place of personal inspection within 7 days of the date of its examination for the purpose of examining, first, the validity, second, the legality and, third, the regularity of its performance. This is a very important element that this law brings forward. There hasn't been such a tool so far. Acts of the rank of regulation or regulations that regulated control issues did not allow this to be triggered. It was just a complaint against the D.A. Thus, the scope of interference in law and freedom by an officer, a municipal guard or a soldier will be subject to scrutiny by an independent court. Although the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal does not directly determine the non-compliance - it was mentioned - the legal basis for the scope and mode of viewing of the contents of the luggage or checking the cargo with the provisions of the Constitution, the explanatory memorandum states that both the personal inspection and the viewing of the contents of the luggage should be subject to an independent judicial review. Therefore, the project proposes similar arrangements for viewing the contents of the luggage and checking the cargo. Such a solution is supported by the same objective of personal inspection and viewing the contents of the luggage or checking the cargoes, as well as in certain cases the need for the said operations to be carried out together. I would also like to refer to the duplicate questions of the two Members concerning the legislative process. I would like to remind you that the project submitted is a commission project. The legislative initiative also has a committee, Mr Wójcik, but please do not question the validity of the implementation of the Constitutional Tribunal's ruling to the draft law, because it strictly implements and implements the rulings of the Tribunal. In the assessment of the Ministry of Home Affairs and Administration, the proposed solutions are, as I mentioned, necessary for the execution of the judgment of the Court by determining the extent of possible interference and by granting judicial review of the legality of the actions in question, the standard of constitutional protection of the rights of the persons to whom they are carried out is increased. At the same time, with a view to the time set by the Court, after which the contested provisions will cease to apply, I call on you to adopt a project beyond the political divisions, while adding, by clarifying that not all of these acts will completely lose that power, we can clarify in detail which, when and to what extent at the next committee meeting. Once again, I wanted to thank you very much for preparing this very important project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-12-sejm-74-1.u391	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present my position on the Commission's draft law amending the Police Act and some other laws included in print no. 3088. Ladies and gentlemen! In accordance with the reasons put forward by the High Chamber, the draft law amending the Police Act and certain other laws constitutes the execution of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 14 December 2017, sygn. Act K 17/14. In view of the content of my decision, it is necessary to align the provisions of a number of laws on the definition of search limits and on the powers of officers to carry out personal checks with the constitutional standards guaranteed by Article. Article 41 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 I, in Article. 47 in conjunction with Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 and Article 3 Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 I and Art. Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 constitutions. High Izbo! In order to fully understand the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal, it is necessary to briefly quote the provisions of the Constitution on which the Tribunal relied. Art. Article 41 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 And the constitution provides everyone with personal immunity and personal freedom. The removal or limitation of freedom may only take place in accordance with the rules laid down in the Act and in the manner set out therein. In addition, on the basis of Article. 47 Constitutions each have the right to legal protection of private, family life, honor and good name and to decide on their personal life. On the other hand, restrictions on the exercise of constitutional freedoms and rights, quoting Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 Constitutions may be laid down only in the law and only if they are necessary in a democratic state to ensure its security or public order, or to protect the environment, health and public morality, or the freedoms and rights of others. At the same time, these restrictions must not affect the essence of freedom and rights. It should be added that in accordance with the standards set out in Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 I Constitution Everyone has the right to a fair and public hearing without undue delay by a competent, independent, impartial and independent court. In addition, according to the wording of the Article. Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 Constitutions, the law must not close anybody's judicial path to the investigation of violated freedoms or rights. Ladies and gentlemen! In the spirit of my provisions, the Constitutional Tribunal ruled that Article. 219 § 2 of the Law of 6 June 1997 Code of Criminal Procedure and Art. 44 § 2 of the Law of 24 August 2001 in so far as it provides for the search of a person, without specifying the boundaries of that search, is incompatible with Article. Article 41 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 I and Art. 47 in conjunction with Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 Constitutions. In addition, the Court held that the provisions of the Police Act, the Border Guard Act, the Municipal Guards, the Military Gendarmery and the Military Enforcement Authorities, the Internal Security Agency and the Intelligence Agency, the Military Counterintelligence Service and the Military Intelligence Service, the Central Anti-corruption Office and the Government Protection Office, which have already lost legal power to the extent that they entitle officials, municipal guards and soldiers to carry out personal checks without specifying the limits of that control, are incompatible with Article. Article 41 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 I and Art. 47 in conjunction with Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Constitution, and in so far as they do not provide for judicial review of the legality of personal checks, they are incompatible with Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 I and Art. Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 constitutions. High Izbo! Freedoms and rights enshrined in the Charters of the Constitution are linked to the closest values of the natural and inalienable dignity of the human person. The Constitution states that it is inviolable and that its respect and protection are the responsibility of the public authorities. The proper legal protection of constitutional rights in the event of searches and personal checks is therefore necessary and is the responsibility of Parliament. Ladies and gentlemen! Given the above-mentioned position and the fact that the draft law fulfils the obligations imposed on Parliament to implement the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal, I find that it requires a substantive debate, and on behalf of the Modern State, I request that the draft be referred to the committee for further work. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-12-sejm-74-1.u425	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! By acting as the person representing the applicants, I have the honour to present a draft law amending the Act amending the Law on excise duty and the Customs Law contained in the Seym printing No. 3084. The Act provides for a transitional regulation which, if adopted by the High House, would allow the application by 31 December 2019 of a paper delivery document for movements into the territory of the country outside the suspension of excise duty procedure, first, excise goods exempt from excise duty on account of their intended use and, secondly, excise goods listed in Annex 2 to the excise duty Act taxed at zero excise duty on account of their use. The regulation responds to the expectations of operators indicating difficulties in adapting to handling movements in the EMCS system, which may result in a reduction or slowdown in the supply of excise goods, inter alia. exempt from excise duty aviation fuel or LPG gas, but also an extensive catalogue of other products, which are described in the manner set out in the previous sentence. According to the applicants, the project has a positive socio-economic impact. First of all, we are not obliged to monitor the timetable for introducing certain tax regulations, especially when we have good tax revenues. As far as excise duty is concerned, enforcement after October shows an increase in excise revenue of PLN 3 billion, year by year. It is worth remembering that excise revenue is the second largest after VAT revenue and is planned for 70 billion in the current year. The draft budget law for the previous year assumed a revenue increase of 1 billion compared to the current year, but this implementation, which was recorded in this phase of implementation of the budget law, is satisfactory. There is therefore reason to believe that due to the high involvement of the business community - this applies to many operators who carry out services in this area - their requests have been taken into account and heard by the High House at, I hope, the favour of the Ministry of Finance. I therefore ask the High House and parliamentary clubs to take a kind approach to this project and to refer it to work in the Public Finance Committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-12-sejm-74-1.u427	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 1 paragraph 2 of the proposal introduced provisions on the application of the existing provisions to movements outside the suspension procedure of excise duty, excise goods exempt from excise duty on account of their intended use and excise goods taxed at zero rate on account of their intended use. Paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: 3 refers to the provision of paragraph. 1 shall not apply to movements outside the procedure of suspension of excise duty on coal products intended for heating purposes. According to the grounds referred to by the applicant, this draft law is neutral for the state budget and for the budgets of local government units. On the other hand, the project has a positive impact on the activities of micro, small and medium-sized enterprises by introducing a transitional period during which entrepreneurs, in addition to the new provisions providing for the use of an electronic delivery document, can also apply the current regulations providing for the use of a paper delivery document. This draft law is compatible with EU law. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support this bill in the vote. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-12-sejm-74-1.u457	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats, I have the honour to present our position on the report of the committee on the parliamentary draft law amending the Law on notaries (prints Nos 2356 and 3058). This parliamentary draft of amendments to the Law on notaria implies that the obligation of a notary to serve directly is removed from it. The two-Article amendment eliminates the rules on direct service. This change, following Parliament's adoption and after the President's signature, is to enter into force 14 days after its announcement. In the assessment of the authors of the project, the current rules are completely unnecessary, as in practice other more effective means of sending letters are used than directly by a notary. According to the justification given in the statistics of the Justice Ministry, this mode was used only 200 times a year on a scale of 3.5 thousand. Polish notaries. In 2017, only 157 statements were made by notaries throughout the country. In the course of the work on the project, the article was also deleted. 79 point 3 of the Law on notariat. This article, among the activities performed by notaries, mentioned the delivery of letters. There is also a provision stating that the existing provisions may be applied in the event of a party requesting service of a letter directly. The reason for the deletion of articles is also to avoid EU law, which forces notaries to serve directly all writings, but only if such an obligation is enshrined in national law. By deleting these two articles from the Polish Law on notariat, this obligation will be avoided. According to the opinion to the draft, the President of the National Notarial Council, the actions identified in the repealed legislation are not of greater practical value since, in view of the existence of alternative effective mechanisms for the service of documents, they are completely unnecessary. It's hard to disagree with that opinion.

In its 2017 report, the Council adopted Decision (CFSP) The NI concluded that the old-timers continued to carry out incorrect and unlawful supervision through incorrect performance of their obligations in carrying out periodic inspections of operators operating vehicle control stations, failure to verify the accuracy of the technical tests carried out by the diagnostics and, in the case of such checks, the lack of questioning of irregularities, incorrect documentation of the conduct of checks, lack of audit recommendations and enforcement of their implementation, the lack of proper use of the legal instruments for the exercise of supervision set out in the legislation. The consequences of faulty surveillance by the elderly are: reducing the level of road safety, increasing the risk to human life and health, the illegal operation of vehicle control stations without the required legal certification by the Director of Transport Technical Supervision, the spread of impunity among diagnostics, hiring diagnostics of vehicle control stations with valid judgments, the entry into service of vehicles in poor technical condition and the issuing of registrations of a technical examination not in conformity with the facts. As far as these two people are concerned, I suspect it will be a much higher cost than in transport, in TDT. In order to be able to do this in all these old places, we would need to hire about 760 employees, and in TDT there will be 320 employees. ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-12-sejm-74-1.u494	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-12-sejm-74-1.u495	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the Government's draft law amending the Aviation Law Act and some other laws, prints No. 2988 and 3065. The basic reason for the government to propose an amendment to the Aviation Law Act is the need to bring national law into line with the rules adopted by the various bodies of the European Union. This concerns the adaptation of our right to as many as 23 Community acts. The scope of the Act covers areas of concern such as general aviation issues, airport problems, civil aviation security, aviation operations, air personnel, passenger rights, aviation technology, air transport, safety management and air navigation. The project introduces as many as 167 amendments to the Law of 3 July 2002. Aviation law, some of which are heavily expanded, contain completely new chapters and also amends four other laws: the Border Guard, spatial planning and development, plant protection products and State Medical Rescue. It is difficult to respond in a short time to the detailed content of the new entries. I will simply say that it will probably be important for passengers to introduce an institution of the passenger's right to be appointed for 5 years in order to resolve passenger disputes more quickly out of court, including the enforcement of their claims without referring to universal courts, where cases go on for a very long time. A completely new chapter in the Act introduces provisions on complaint proceedings, which increases the chances of amicable handling a large part of such cases. The draft law also lays down the limitation period for a passenger's property claims up to 1 year after the flight is carried out. At the same time, it harmonises European law with the case law and the argument of the Supreme Court in Poland. The project also regulates other areas, e.g. sets limits on the rates of air charges which the Minister for Transport will not be able to exceed when they are defined in the Regulation. It then regulates the working time of helicopter crews and emergency medical services helicopters, providing them with 24 hours of rest. It amends the definition of the aviation security service, thus making the civil aviation security system transparent, including the possibility to capture and transfer to the Police or Border Guard persons disturbing public order by a protective formation. Of course, all security oversight and control obligations are left to the managing authority of the airport. The Act contains four annexes describing sanctions in tabular form, inter alia. for breach of European rules on basic standards for civil aviation security or for breach of the rules governing the operation of air services within the Community. Art. 18 guarantees that the existing implementing provisions are maintained until the new regulations are adopted, but not later than 18 months after the entry into force of the amending law, except for two provisions of the Article. 1 which will enter into force from 1 January 2019. This means that there will be no legal gap in the law implementing the law. The project is, of course, compatible with European Union law, which is practically implementing European solutions in Poland. Ladies and gentlemen! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the proposal presented to the High House.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-12-sejm-74-1.u511	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! As far as the limitation period is concerned, that is - we are talking about the limitation period which results from EU rules - this annual limitation period. This is the deadline for EU legislation. As for the question about the involvement of the Civil Aviation Authority in spatial planning, the change we are introducing is actually only related to the sealing of the system. The Civil Aviation Authority is already participating in the planning of the areas surrounding the airport, but we have to face the fact that a large part of the areas around the airports are areas for which there are no spatial planning plans. In other words, the construction process is based on decisions referred to colloquially as WZ. Leaving such a decision without the participation of the Civil Aviation Authority would seriously jeopardise the functioning of airports, as we can imagine a situation in which, with regard to the route on the extension of runways, there would be a decision to allow construction, for example. 15-story building. This, of course, is an extreme case. However, we are not really talking about a significant change in the system here, we are talking about sealing the system and taking into account that planning does not cover a very important part of the area near airports. This is the justification for this change. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-12-sejm-74-1.u513	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, I am pleased to present to the High House a report on the work of the Committee on the Government's draft law on the protection of personal data processed in connection with the prevention and fight against print crime No 2989. The committee's report is in print 3089. The subject included in the draft law, which I am presenting to the High House, is very complex. The project consists of several hundred pages with justification and concerns very important issues. First of all, the draft law was based on the need to implement into our Polish legal order the solutions contained in the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2006, which specifically addresses the issue of the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data by competent authorities for the purposes of preventing crime, conducting investigations, detecting and prosecuting criminal offences and the enforcement of penalties, on the free movement of such data within the European Union. The Directive is hereinafter referred to as Directive 2016/680. In the European Union, the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data is one of the most important fundamental rights enshrined in the Charter of Fundamental Rights of the European Union and in the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union. The relevant provisions of these documents provide that each person has the right to the protection of personal data concerning him or her. Therefore, in order to ensure uniform rules in all the Member States of the European Union and in view of the high degree of need for the protection of personal data, it has been decided that new provisions will be drawn up in this area within the European Union, and that is precisely what the relevant directive provides for. At first reading, the Commission decided to refer the project to the subcommittee. Two meetings of this subcommittee took place, at which together with the Legislative Office, with the government party, with Minister Kozłowski, we discussed the draft law extensively with NGOs. In the course of the work, appropriate amendments were made and, following the referral of the subcommittee's report to the committee, the High Committee also addressed the draft in detail. As a result of the discussion and the whole process, the committee requests the High House to adopt the bill contained in the committee's report in print No 3089. Thank you very much on behalf of the committee for the opportunity to present this report.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-12-sejm-74-1.u514	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-12-sejm-74-1.u515	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to refer to the draft law on the protection of personal data processed in connection with the prevention and fight against crime. It is of particular importance as it sets out the legitimate purposes for the processing of personal data within the meaning of the Act, and for this reason the aim of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs - already mentioned by the rapporteur - was to ensure the quality of the proposed rules. The new legislation is not only about giving public authorities a certain discretion in the processing of personal data in relation to the prevention and fight against crime, but also, where such freedom is necessary, a very effective appeal mechanism, which allows for a real control of the legality of the activities carried out by independent authorities, in particular here, the courts must be mentioned. The proposed law ensures that the balance between, on the one hand, the right to privacy and, on the other hand, the necessity for the police and other entities operating in the area of prevention and combating crime, in plain terms, confidentiality in the processing of data in proceedings conducted by authorised authorities. The introduction of new regulations will result in a single legal framework for the processing of personal data. I would like to stress that in the course of the committee's work the proposed provisions have been supplemented by the possibility to appoint a replacement administrator in order to adapt the regulations adopted in the draft amendments to the Act of 10 May 2018 on the protection of personal data. In addition, the rules on fees charged by the administrator for the provision of information and by statutory delegations are clarified. However, in order to avoid interpretational doubts, terminology has also been harmonised during work through consistent use of the same nomenclature, i.e. the public authority of other Member States of the European Union. The provision for delegations to issue a regulation laying down inter alia has been deleted. the way and mode for the Police to share image and sound recording. This applies to Article. 15b of the Police Act. This was followed by a similar solution previously included in the Military Police Act and the Border Guard Act. I would also like to add that, at the stage of the committee's work, a number of changes have been made that have been made to some of the comments made in particular in the Ombudsman's speech - here I would stress the issue of further consistency in the provisions relating to the conditions for the appointment of a personal data inspector - and also to the doubts raised during the work of the special subcommittee by some of your Members - were particularly concerned with the revision of the wording of the article. Article 30 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 6 of the draft law on clarification of the time limits for replying. In view of the above, I recommend on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice the adoption of a draft law on the protection of personal data processed in connection with the prevention and fight against crime. This is not only the implementation of one of the basic requirements of law-making, according to which the determination of powers, obligations and means of action should be at the level of the law, which is directly apparent from the article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Constitution, but also corresponds directly to the article. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the proposed law, which is the implementation of Article. Article 8 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Directive and indicating that the competent authorities process personal data only to the extent necessary to exercise the right or to fulfil the obligation arising from the provision of law. During the course of the work, the specific discussion raised the exemptions set out in Article. 3 projects. It should be clarified that the reason for the exclusion referred to in Article. 3 point 1 of the project is the conceptual scope of the definition of the data set. In the light of doctrine and case law, the data set as defined in Article. 3 (6) of the Directive should meet the following criteria: it must be a set of personal data; personal data in this set must be structured in such a way that the requested information can be searched without the need to search the entire set; the data must be structured according to at least two specific criteria, and these criteria should refer to individuals, so that the collection is ordered in such a way that personal data can be searched without the need to search the entire set. Based on the above Directives, they do not apply to files such as criminal proceedings files because they do not meet the criteria mentioned: files of cases and activities collect various types of information and evidence; evidence collected in files of cases and activities do not form a set of data; they do not constitute a uniform set of data, nor can they be searched according to specific criteria of personal data contained in the evidence. Personal data obtained in the course of proceedings shall, however, be subject to increased protection in relation to the protection provided for by the Directive: in accordance with Article. 156 § 5 The Code of Criminal Procedure, where there is no need to safeguard the proper course of proceedings or the protection of important interests of the State, shall be made available to the parties, defenders, representatives and statutory representatives during the preparatory proceedings, copies or copies shall be made available, and certified copies or copies shall be issued for payment; this right may also be granted to the parties after the investigation has been completed; with the consent of the prosecutor, the file may be made available in exceptional cases to other persons; disclosure of the data from proceedings, depending on the facts, may constitute a criminal offence as set out in Article. 266 or Article 26 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council; 241 of the Penal Code, as well as of the disciplinary breach of Article. 102 Law on the Public Prosecutor's Office or Art. 85 Law on the system of common courts; pursuant to Article. 148a The Code of Criminal Procedure does not include data on the place of residence and the place of work of the victims and witnesses involved. It should be stressed that the Directive provides for the possibility of introducing a different regime relating to criminal proceedings. According to Art. 18 of the Directive, as well as recitals 20 and 49 of the preamble thereto, the national legislature may in such a way establish the protection of personal data collected in criminal proceedings in order to comply with the national procedure governing those proceedings. Special services were also excluded from the proposed law. I will refer here to the Member's question about the Central Anti-corruption Office. I'll quote the article. 1 of the CBA Act, which states: The Central Anti-corruption Office, hereinafter referred to as 'CBA', is established as a special service for combating corruption in public and economic life, in particular in public and local authorities, as well as for combating activities affecting the economic interests of the State. Using this concept, we also refer to the aforementioned term in the area of national security. Continuing - in Art. 2 (2) 3 lit. and the Directive indicates that it does not apply to the processing of personal data within an activity not covered by Union law. In this context, it should be recalled that the Treaty on European Union in Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. The third sentence provides that, in particular, national security remains the exclusive responsibility of each Member State. At the same time, the provision of the Article. 2 (2) 3 lit. and the Directives should be read taking into account recital 14, which clarifies the scope of the Directive. According to recital 14, the provisions of the Directive should not apply to the processing of personal data in the course of activities outside the scope of Union law. At the same time, the notion of activities beyond the scope of... The implementation of the specific statutory tasks of each of these entities is aimed at ensuring national security, as I mentioned earlier. EU law in a large part of the legislation is transferred directly by the implementation of the language of the Directive into the legal order. This is a problem and, of course, a challenge for every legislator. However, it should be noted that, according to the official website of the European Union legal base, 16 countries have so far implemented the Directive. It also requires that work on adapting national legislation to the provisions of the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016 has been affected by the deadline for submitting a draft law to the Sejm. In fact, the draft law at issue directly uses the solutions adopted in the Personal Data Protection Act of 10 May 2018 and defines the supervisory authority, the President of the Office for Personal Data Protection, which is identical to the supervisory authority set out in that Act, carrying out supervisory tasks under Regulation 2016/679, which is similarly defined by the Directive. The President of the Office will also be able to carry out checks on the processing of personal data using the procedures laid down in Chapter 9 of the Personal Data Protection Act for this purpose. This solution constitutes an additional guarantee element, bringing both personal data protection systems together in the maximum possible way, while taking into account their specific characteristics, which is regulated by this law. ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-12-sejm-74-1.u535	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of Kukiz15 on the draft law on the protection of personal data processed in relation to the prevention and fight against crime. This proposal is justified by the need to implement into the Polish legal order the solutions contained in the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016. One reason for this was the need to ensure a consistent and sufficiently high level of protection of personal data of individuals, as well as to facilitate the exchange of personal data between the competent authorities of the Member States to ensure effective cooperation in criminal matters and police cooperation. The aim is to ensure that data can be transferred to a third country, provided that the purpose is to prosecute crimes while ensuring an adequate level of data protection by the third country. This includes the processing of personal data by competent authorities for the purposes of preventing, investigating, detecting and prosecuting criminal offences or the execution of criminal penalties, including protection against and the prevention of threats to public security. One of the main objectives of the project is to ensure a balance between the right to privacy and the need for the police and other actors in the field of preventing and combating confidentiality crime when processing data in proceedings conducted by the competent authorities. The Kukiz Club15 accepts the project's arguments and will vote in favour of the proposed bill on the protection of personal data processed in connection with the prevention and fight against crime. However, we would like to stress that the position of the club stems solely from our conviction that there is a need for legislation on the protection of personal data. However, this need arises - and we are fully aware of it - directly from the reckless introduction of legislation that has opened wide the door to surveillance of our lives and the collection of sensitive data by various kinds of services. Typically, when we first adopt such legislation, we are now forced to minimise its effects by introducing further legislation, this time ensuring an adequate level of protection of personal data and sensitive data. This would not be necessary if access to data for various services was introduced with thought, responsibility and after an analysis of the consequences of these changes at an appropriate level. Meanwhile, everything is done in the opposite way. First of all, there is a law that allows for almost unlimited access to our data, especially sensitive data, and second, another legislation aimed at reducing the risk that these data will not necessarily be used as we wish. This is a practice that makes it very sceptical to look at all the legislative changes in this area. As the proverb says: a wise Pole after injury. However, the Kukiz Club15 considers that proper protection of personal data is a priority and a basis for the proper functioning of the society and for not putting citizens at risk of various abuses. In view of the above, as I said, we will vote in favour of the proposal submitted.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-12-sejm-74-1.u559	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I'd like to refer to the questions and the bill. Well, there's nothing new under this law. We're not moving in, we're just changing what happened. As far as this fee is concerned, up to 1 MWh, this is a fee which amounts to 12.58 in 2018 and will amount to 1.58 in 2019, which means that the annual household burden will be 3.95 in this respect. It changes significantly, because so far, if we look at the annual household burden, it was almost 32 PLN. It changes to 3.95 as part of this new support system. And this new support system is aimed at developing as widely as possible the energy and heat system in the country for consumers connected to heating systems, but also at improving air quality. These are two basic things. I would like to point out that this improvement in air quality is also included very strongly, because support will only be granted to those units whose emission factor does not exceed 450 kg/MWh in the case of electricity and heat generation. That is why we hope and strive for a system that promotes everything new, to develop and also to reach smaller towns, districts or municipalities, where there are very old heating plants. Renovating them on the heat plant will make it possible for us to produce electricity in a distributed system. This law has indeed been made for a long time, but nevertheless it is well prepared. In parallel with its preparation, we had discussions with the European Commission, so practically all issues related to its formation have been negotiated and corrected on an ongoing basis. That is why we can say today that the law has been formally pre-noticed, and in fact a notification, because everything points out that, after the act has been passed, it will be notified by the European Commission in the form in which it is adopted. The recent amendments that have actually been made are due to our needs for the implementation of this law in the right way. I would also like to stress that this law does not introduce any additional tribute and reduces the existing one. It actually reduces the levy which was established by the States in 2014. Referring to Mr Maciejewski's statement that we are seeking to maximise the fees, I say that this is not true. That's minimizing charges. We aim to ensure that the fee is used as efficiently as possible, so help will be given to those units that will deliver a minimum of 70% utility heat from cogeneration to the public heating network. All other units will receive support proportional to the share of heat introduced into the grid in the heat generated. This is a very important relationship, because it is essentially a matter of ensuring that the emerging heat plants do not focus only on electricity generation, but also on using as much heat as possible, so to participate in improving air quality, because this improvement is needed. We all need clean air. As regards renewable energy sources that were mentioned on this occasion, auctions took place for them. This year, renewable wind energy sources, photovoltaics and biogas plants are emerging, which also receive support and for which a special auction basket was created. Support must reach where it is needed, i.e. we support those power plants that climb financially. We look at this very closely and prepare the bill in such a way that support is not provided by chance. These things are extremely important, which is why... On the other hand, with regard to the words of Mr Wolf, who, during the debate on the bill, focused on the issue of coal gasification, I would like to stress that the system of gasification of underground coal, after experience in the mine of Wieczorek and not only, is not being adopted anywhere in the world. That's why we want to focus on trying to gas the ground. That's why he's in the right process right now... After consulting technology owners, i.e. with the Japanese, the first power plant in Poland, or actually in Europe, is being built, which will be powered by gas obtained from gasification of coal in a closed system - at the Bogdanka mine. It's being prepared by Enea's energy group. We consider this to be very important and necessary piloting. Mr Pyzik spoke about the energy balance. Here the support system is and will be financed by the cogeneration fee. I've talked about this before. On the other hand, actions which will be linked to this, namely preparatory and systemic actions, will be financed by the Ministry of Energy and URE. It finances the Treasury. Mr Romecki focused on the exclusion of RES. There is no exclusion of RES in Poland. Of course, the system has changed, different distances have been adopted, but it turns out that the distances of places that are suitable meet all the requirements necessary for the construction of renewable energy sources in Poland are many, therefore these auctions have and will take place. There's no disfellowshipping here. As in the case of biogas plants, they have a special basket and are contracted under auction. It is not expected that there will be any difficulties here. That's what we're trying to do. Personally, I am pleased with prosumer development - with just over 100 MW we already have over 300 MW in the system of installed prosumer capacity. This is developing very dynamically - 45 thousand. installation. They come clearly, so this distributed system is very important and it seems that it needs a little more promotion, because it does not reach all those who might be interested yet. As regards the answer to Mrs Skowronska, I have already said: This fee is not a new one, the next one is only a changed and reduced fee compared to the previous one. In this area, this type of support system is needed, because in the system of distributed receptions, longer district heating networks, without support... This type of cogeneration, which at the same time gives it a major contribution to improving air quality especially in smaller towns where low emissions are very dangerous, is important. So we're trying to do it in a way that makes it as cost-effective as possible for the energy user when it comes to the charges. Mr Gadowski... We have indeed tabled these amendments. They always result from the fact that the law is clarified, but thanks to this I am sure that we will be able to obtain its notification immediately, also actually in the provisions of the law there is nothing, no article or any provision which would be unknown to the European Commission and which would not have initial acceptance. However, this reaching takes a long time, but in this area we are effective because we are able to convince the European Commission in some way, and sometimes also to take into account the European Commission's reasons, which are right, because with this type of negotiation experienced workers who are in the European Commission can also help a lot. As far as consultations are concerned, in view of time, the law has been subject to any consultation required in accordance with the legislative procedure, none have been omitted. All industry, local government, etc. - there have been consultations, we have had a whole consultation system, so in this area, we are okay on this issue. If necessary, we serve the protocols of these meetings. [[Will we reach 15% by 2020?]] I think, and I'm pretty sure I do. As regards the systems that we have already launched, also notified and agreed with the European Commission on amendments to the RES Act, which is fully accepted, I am convinced that there should be no problems. Now there's the shape... As far as the policy is concerned, as regards the fact that energy is increasingly expensive in Poland: The prices that sellers issue are, as far as the system of natural persons is concerned, approved by URE. As far as possibilities are concerned, everyone chooses their own option, because there is also the sale of energy in the negotiating system, i.e. on the basis of contracts. The local authorities shall publish appropriate tender procedures. On the other hand, citizens are within the regulatory price framework, so sales prices are approved by the Energy Regulatory Office and are also evaluated. As far as prices are concerned in CHP plants, they are also assessed by the municipalities' councils, also from the social side is full and proper supervision. It must also be stated that there is full transparency in this area. There's full transparency everywhere. And so much for answers to questions, actually. I would like to thank you for the fact that the committee has undertaken to proceed quickly on this bill. Indeed, a significant flaw in its preparation was the departure from the financing of industrial heat and power plants, which produced energy for their own needs, because they actually absorbed more than half of the resources and for this reason there was less resources for the development of the electricity and power networks in Poland. Some of these power plants have already found their place in the power market, and I think the others - these large industrial ones - will also find their place in the power market. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Then the president of the city presented the planned schedule for its implementation, and the committee accepted it. The previous government, PO-PSL, adopted in 2015 the Road and Motorways Construction Programme until 2023. Then the Starogard ring road was on the list of primary construction of the 48th position bypasses, with the funding provided. The PiS government changed the road and highway construction program and the Starogard Gdansk ring road was located at 147th place of all roads to be completed, and only 118 roads are provided with funding. Thus, the Starogard ring road is currently ranked 29th on the reserve list, i.e. without funding provided by 2023. As long as the inhabitants of Starogard Gdański expected the investment was simply withdrawn. I'm asking you, why? Until this summer, the Ministry of Infrastructure has given a negative opinion on the current option, saying that they want a cheaper option. Thus, designers are again involved, as this involves redesigning the route over a distance of approx. 4 km. Many times, both during meetings in this city and by telephone or e-mail, residents ask me to start working on this ring road of Starogard Gdansk as soon as possible. I therefore appeal to the PiS Members, especially those from the electoral district of Starogard Gdański, for a change of opinion and support for this necessary investment, which I wish the people of Starogard Gdański. After all, it is enough to go to Starogard just once to see how much this city needs this bypass. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-12-sejm-74-1.u572	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! A very important press conference took place today in the Seym. It was a conference involving two Poles living in the UK, two parents, activists of children's rights. We have a huge problem in the British Isles: we take away Poles, Polish parents each year, a huge number of children. It is estimated that around 300 children are received on different pretexts each year in the United Kingdom. That's the number. probably much larger than e.g. in Germany and Norway, which is very often heard, but hardly anyone talks about it. While the problem of Norway and Germany is often raised and some action is taken against Poles living there, in the UK there is actually nothing done in this direction to safeguard the rights of parents and their children. Children are picked up under any pretext, actually for no reason, and this is happening more and more in a way that is even illegal, as is said in these environments. Worst of all, Poles, Polish citizens living there, are actually deprived of any protection of Polish diplomatic services. They can't actually count on any help from our diplomats. The situation is absolutely tragic. Parents are intimidated, scared, they don't know their rights, they don't know how to fight for their children. Children are being picked up, but the worst part is that there's some sort of trafficking in human beings, because for each child received, special agencies receive about 27,000. Pounds. In short, it has become a great practice to collect and get huge money for every child that has been taken away. It is simply business and in this business earns various organizations, even different public agendas, with the famous Social Service, called the modern SS, headed by. This can't go on. We ask for actions of the Polish government, Polish foreign services, Polish diplomatic services, we ask for actions in the interest and for the benefit of these parents. The scale of the problem is huge, and Poles are left to themselves. Polish children are being picked up and it is very, very hard to recover. In fact, no parent from the UK, no Pole can be calm because, as I said before, there are cases when actually without reason or based on fake evidence and pseudo-causes these children are received and transferred first to foster families and then even for adoption, and often for example. to gay families. This problem needs to be solved. As far as other countries are concerned, such protection is possible. Countries that claim their citizens are working very effectively in this area. They work very effectively, for example. Czech Republic. Since the Czech government and the Czech foreign services have taken an interest in this problem, the Czech children are not actually taken away. Slovakia works even better, much more effectively. The paradox was that sometimes Czech lawyers and Czech diplomatic services help Poles more than our own. It would be best to create some special fund at the embassy in the UK, with those diplomatic services, which would enable the employment of special lawyers who will defend Poles against taking their children away. There are a number of different actions, different ways that can be taken and applied to put an end to this and, above all, it is no longer possible to remain silent that this problem does not exist. The problem is very big. Unfortunately, this is tragic, because it affects the most vulnerable, the youngest, i.e. children. I appeal to the government, I appeal to the Minister of Foreign Affairs, I appeal to the Minister of the Interior to finally address this problem and stop being deaf to this great tragedy of Poles living on the Islands. Thank you very much.
